ENESPT

Vajrakula

El Sūtra de la Flor de Loto del Dharma Maravilloso

La segunda parte de la trilogía de escrituras del Sutra del Loto

Traducido por el Acharya Vajraprajna

Comenzar a leer

Capítulo I

Introducción

Este capítulo aún está en traducción al castellano; se muestra provisoriamente en inglés.

Thus have I heard:

At one time, the Buddha was dwelling in the City of Royal Palaces, on Mount Gṛdhrakūṭa, together with a great assembly of twelve thousand bhikṣus. All were arhats whose outpourings were exhausted, who had no further afflictions, had attained their own benefit, had exhausted the bonds of existence, and whose minds had attained mastery. Their names were: Ājñātakauṇḍinya, Mahākāśyapa, Uruvilvākāśyapa, Gayākāśyapa, Nadīkāśyapa, Śāriputra, Mahāmaudgalyāyana, Mahākātyāyana, Aniruddha, Kapphiṇa, Gavāmpati, Revata, Pilindavatsa, Bakkula, Mahākausthila, Nanda, Sundarananda, Pūrṇamaitrāyaṇīputra, Subhūti, Ānanda, and Rāhula—such great arhats known to the assembly.

Furthermore, there were two thousand practitioners in training and beyond training. The bhikṣuṇī Mahāprajāpatī was present with her six thousand attendants. The bhikṣuṇī Yaśodharā, mother of Rāhula, was also present with her attendants.

There were eighty thousand Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas, all irreversible from anuttarasamyaksambodhi. All had attained dhāraṇī, possessed pleasant and unhindered eloquence, and turned the irreversible wheel of Dharma. They had made offerings to immeasurable hundreds of thousands of Buddhas, planted roots of virtue under those Buddhas, and were constantly praised by the Buddhas. They cultivated their bodies with compassion, skillfully entered the Buddha’s wisdom, penetrated great wisdom, and arrived at the other shore. Their fame was heard universally throughout immeasurable worlds, and they were able to liberate countless hundreds of thousands of wandering beings.

Their names were: Mañjuśrī Bodhisattva, Avalokiteśvara Bodhisattva, Mahāsthāmaprāpta Bodhisattva, Nityodyukta Bodhisattva, Anikṣiptadhura Bodhisattva, Ratnapāṇi Bodhisattva, Bhaiṣajyarāja Bodhisattva, Pradānaśūra Bodhisattva, Ratnacandra Bodhisattva, Candraprabha Bodhisattva, Pūrṇacandra Bodhisattva, Mahābala Bodhisattva, Anantabala Bodhisattva, Trailokavikrāmin Bodhisattva, Bhadrapāla Bodhisattva, Maitreya Bodhisattva, Ratnākara Bodhisattva, and Nāyaka Bodhisattva—such Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas, eighty thousand in all.

At that time, Śakra Devānām Indra was present with his retinue of twenty thousand sons of devas. There were also the son of devas Candradeva, the son of devas Gandhadeva, the son of devas Prabhāketu, and the Four Heavenly Kings with their retinue of ten thousand sons of devas. The son of devas Īśvara and the son of devas Maheśvara were present with their retinue of thirty thousand sons of devas. The lord of the Sahā world, Brahmā King Śikhin Mahābrahmā and Jyotiṣprabha Mahābrahmā were present with their retinue of twelve thousand sons of devas.

There were eight Naga kings: Nanda Naga King, Upananda Naga King, Sāgara Naga King, Vāsuki Naga King, Takṣaka Naga King, Anavatapta Naga King, Manasvin Naga King, and Utpala Naga King, each with several hundred thousand attendants.

There were four kiṃnara kings: Dharma Kiṃnara King, Sudharma Kiṃnara King, Mahādharma Kiṃnara King, and Dharmadhara Kiṃnara King, each with several hundred thousand attendants.

There were four gandharva kings: Gīta Gandharva King, Gītarava Gandharva King, Madhura Gandharva King, and Madhurasvaraghoṣa Gandharva King, each with several hundred thousand attendants.

There were four asura kings: Bali Asura King, Kharaskandha Asura King, Vemacitrin Asura King, and Rāhu Asura King, each with several hundred thousand attendants.

There were four garuḍa kings: Mahātejas Garuḍa King, Mahākāya Garuḍa King, Mahāpūrṇa Garuḍa King, and Ṛddhimat Garuḍa King, each with several hundred thousand attendants.

King Ajātaśatru, son of Vaidehī, was present with several hundred thousand attendants. Each bowed to the Buddha’s feet and withdrew to sit on one side.

At that time, the World-Honored One, surrounded by the fourfold assembly, received offerings, reverence, veneration, and praise. For the sake of the Bodhisattvas, he expounded the Mahāyāna sūtra named Innumerable Meanings, a teaching for Bodhisattvas and guarded in the mindfulness of the Buddhas.

When the Buddha finished expounding this sūtra, he sat cross-legged and entered the samādhi of the Abode of Innumerable Meanings, his body and mind unmoving. At that time, heaven rained down māndārava flowers, mahā-māndārava flowers, mañjūṣaka flowers, and mahā-mañjūṣaka flowers, scattering them over the Buddha and the great assembly. The universal Buddha-worlds shook in six ways.

Then in the assembly, the bhikṣus, bhikṣuṇīs, upāsakas, upāsikās, devas, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kiṃnaras, mahoragas, humans, non-humans, petty kings, and wheel-turning sage kings—this entire great assembly—attained what they had never experienced before. Joyful with joined palms, they single-mindedly gazed upon the Buddha.

Then the Buddha radiated a light from the white hair between his eyebrows, illuminating eighteen thousand worlds to the east without omission, reaching downward to the Avīci Hell and upward to the Akaniṣṭha Heaven. From this world, all the wandering beings of the six destinies in those lands were fully seen; the present Buddhas of those lands were seen, and the sūtra teachings expounded by those Buddhas were heard. The bhikṣus, bhikṣuṇīs, upāsakas, and upāsikās of those lands who practiced the path and attained realization were also seen.

Furthermore, the Bodhisattva Mahāsattvas were seen practicing the Bodhisattva path through various causal conditions, various faiths and understandings, and various appearances. The Buddhas entering parinirvāṇa were seen. After those Buddhas entered parinirvāṇa, seven-jeweled stūpas erected with their relics were also seen.

At that time, Maitreya Bodhisattva had this thought: Now the World-Honored One displays signs of spiritual transformations; for what cause is there this auspicious sign? Now the Buddha, the World-Honored One, has entered samādhi; this is an inconceivable, rare occurrence. Whom should I ask? Who can answer this?

He further thought: This Mañjuśrī, son of the Dharma King, has already attended upon and made offerings to past immeasurable Buddhas; he must have seen such rare signs. I will now ask him.

At that time, the bhikṣus, bhikṣuṇīs, upāsakas, upāsikās, devas, nāgas, ghosts, and spirits all had this thought: Regarding this sign of the Buddha’s radiant light and spiritual powers, whom should we now ask?

Then Maitreya Bodhisattva, wishing to resolve his own doubt and observing the thoughts of the fourfold assembly of bhikṣus, bhikṣuṇīs, upāsakas, and upāsikās, as well as the gathered assembly of devas, nāgas, ghosts, and spirits, addressed Mañjuśrī:

For what cause is there this auspicious sign of spiritual power, emitting a great light illuminating eighteen thousand lands to the east, so that the adornments of those Buddha realms are fully seen?

Thereupon, Maitreya Bodhisattva, wishing to restate this meaning, inquired in gāthās:

Mañjuśrī! For what reason does the Guide,

From the white curl between his eyebrows,

Radiate a vast light universally illuminating?

Raining down māndārava

And mañjūṣaka flowers,

With sandalwood scented breezes

Delighting the minds of the assembly?

By this causal condition,

The earth is completely adorned and purified,

And this world

Shakes in six ways.

At that time the fourfold assembly

Are all filled with joy,

Body and mind delighted,

Attaining what they never had before.

The light from between his eyebrows

Illuminates the eastern direction,

Eighteen thousand lands,

All turning golden in color,

From the Avīci Hell

Upward to the Peak of Existence.

Within all those worlds,

The wandering beings of the six destinies,

The destinations of their births and deaths,

The karmic conditions of good and evil,

And their retributions fine or ugly,

Are all seen from here.

Furthermore, one beholds the Buddhas,

The holy masters and lions,

Expounding the scriptural texts,

Subtle, sublime, and foremost.

Their voices are pure,

Emitting gentle sounds,

Teaching the Bodhisattvas

Countless hundreds of millions,

With deep and subtle Brahma-sounds

Causing people to rejoice in hearing them.

Each within their own worlds

Expounds the correct Dharma

Through various causal conditions.

By immeasurable parables,

They illuminate the Buddha-Dharma

To awaken wandering beings.

If a person encounters suffering,

Weary of aging, sickness, and death,

They speak to them of nirvāṇa,

Exhausting the limits of suffering.

If a person has merit,

Having made offerings to Buddhas,

Aspiring to seek the supreme Dharma,

They speak to them of Conditioned Awakening.

If there are Buddha-children

Cultivating various practices,

Seeking unexcelled wisdom,

They speak to them of the pure path.

Mañjuśrī!

Dwelling here,

I see and hear such things

And thousands of millions of occurrences,

Vast numbers like these,

Which I will now briefly state.

I see in those lands

Bodhisattvas like Ganges sands,

Through various causal conditions,

Seeking the Buddha path.

Some practice giving

Of gold, silver, and coral,

Pearls and mani jewels,

Mother-of-pearl and agate,

Diamonds and various treasures,

Servants, carriages,

And palanquins adorned with jewels,

Joyfully giving them away.

Dedicating them to the Buddha path,

They vow to attain this vehicle,

Foremost in the three realms,

Praised by all the Buddhas.

There are Bodhisattvas who give

Precious carriages drawn by four steeds,

With railings and canopies,

And decorated canopies.

Again I see Bodhisattvas

Giving their flesh, hands, and feet,

As well as wives and children,

Seeking the unexcelled path.

Again I see Bodhisattvas

Giving heads, eyes, and bodies

With joy and delight,

Seeking the Buddha’s wisdom.

Mañjuśrī!

I see various kings

Going to where the Buddhas dwell,

Inquiring of the unexcelled path,

Forthwith abandoning joyful lands,

Palaces, ministers, and consorts,

Shaving off beard and hair,

To put on the Dharma robes.

Some see Bodhisattvas

Becoming bhikṣus,

Dwelling alone in quiet leisure,

Delighting in reciting scriptures.

Again I see Bodhisattvas,

Vigorous and heroic,

Entering deep mountains

To contemplate the Buddha path.

Again I see those free of desires

Constantly dwelling in emptiness and leisure,

Deeply cultivating dhyāna meditation,

Attaining the five spiritual superknowledges.

Again I see Bodhisattvas

Resting in meditation with joined palms,

With thousands of millions of verses

Praising the Dharma Kings.

Again I see Bodhisattvas

Of deep wisdom and firm resolve,

Able to question the Buddhas,

Hearing and upholding all.

Again I see Buddha-children

Endowed with meditation and wisdom,

With immeasurable parables

Expounding the Dharma for the assembly.

Joyfully speaking the Dharma,

Transforming the Bodhisattvas,

Shattering the hosts of Maras,

And striking the Dharma drum.

Again I see Bodhisattvas

Silent and tranquil,

Revered by devas and Nagas,

Yet not finding joy in it.

Again I see Bodhisattvas

Dwelling in forests, emitting light,

Relieving the suffering of hells,

Causing them to enter the Buddha path.

Again I see Buddha-children

Who have never slept,

Walking in the forests,

Diligently seeking the Buddha path.

Again I see those endowed with precepts,

Flawless in deportment,

Pure like precious jewels,

To seek the Buddha path.

Again I see Buddha-children

Dwelling in the power of patience,

When people of arrogant pride

Abuse and strike them,

Able to endure it all,

To seek the Buddha path.

Again I see Bodhisattvas

Free from frivolous laughter

And foolish companions,

Drawing close to the wise,

Single-mindedly removing distraction,

Collecting their thoughts in mountain forests

For hundreds of thousands of millions of years,

To seek the Buddha path.

Some see Bodhisattvas

With delicious foods and drinks,

And a hundred kinds of medicinal broths,

Giving to the Buddha and Saṅgha.

Renowned robes of superior cloth,

Worth thousands and millions,

Or priceless garments,

Giving to the Buddha and Saṅgha.

Thousands of millions of varieties

Of sandalwood precious dwellings,

And exquisite bedding,

Giving to the Buddha and Saṅgha.

Pure gardens and forests,

With lush flowers and fruits,

Flowing springs and bathing ponds,

Giving to the Buddha and Saṅgha.

Givings such as these,

Of various subtle kinds,

Rejoicing without weariness,

Seeking the unexcelled path.

There are Bodhisattvas

Speaking the Dharma of tranquil extinction,

With various instructions

Teaching countless wandering beings.

Some see Bodhisattvas

Contemplating the nature of all dharmas

As having no dual marks,

Just like empty space.

Again I see Buddha-children

With minds having no attachment,

Using this wonderful wisdom

To seek the unexcelled path.

Mañjuśrī!

Furthermore, there are Bodhisattvas

Who, after the Buddha’s extinction,

Make offerings to the relics.

Again I see Buddha-children

Constructing stūpas and temples,

Countless as the sands of the Ganges,

Adorning the lands.

Precious stūpas lofty and sublime,

Five thousand yojanas high,

Their length and breadth equal,

Two thousand yojanas wide.

Each stūpa and shrine

Has a thousand banners and streamers,

Beaded curtains hung all around,

Precious bells chiming in harmony.

All devas, Nagas, and spirits,

Humans and non-humans,

With incense, flowers, and music,

Constantly make offerings.

Mañjuśrī!

All these Buddha-children,

To make offerings to the relics,

Adorn the stūpas and shrines;

The lands are naturally

Extraordinarily fine and wonderful,

Like the heavenly King of Trees

With its flowers blossoming open.

The Buddha emits a single light,

And I and the assembled gathering

Behold these lands,

Variously extraordinary and wonderful.

The spiritual powers of the Buddhas

And their wisdom are exceedingly rare;

Emitting a single pure light,

It illuminates immeasurable lands.

We, seeing this,

Have attained what we never had before.

Buddha-child Mañjuśrī!

We pray you will resolve the assembly’s doubts.

The fourfold assembly looks up with hope,

Gazing upon you and me.

For what cause does the World-Honored One

Emit this radiant light?

Buddha-child, answer in due time;

Resolve our doubts and make us rejoice.

What benefit is brought forth

By displaying this radiant light?

That wonderful Dharma the Buddha attained

Sitting in the place of awakening—

Does he wish to expound it now?

Or is he about to bestow prophecies?

To display all the Buddha-lands,

Adorned and pure with many jewels,

And to reveal all the Buddhas—

This is no small cause.

Mañjuśrī, you must know,

The fourfold assembly, Nagas, and spirits

Gaze upon you, the virtuous one:

What is it that will be expounded?

Then Mañjuśrī addressed Maitreya Bodhisattva Mahāsattva and all the great beings:

Good children! As I consider it, now the Buddha, the World-Honored One, wishes to expound the great Dharma, rain down the great Dharma rain, blow the great Dharma conch, strike the great Dharma drum, and proclaim the great Dharma meaning. Good children! In the presence of past Buddhas, I have seen this auspicious sign. Having emitted this light, they immediately expounded the great Dharma. Therefore, you should know that the present Buddha displaying this light is also like this; wishing to cause wandering beings to hear and know the Dharma difficult to believe in all the world, he displays this auspicious sign.

Good children! In the past, immeasurable, boundless, inconceivable, incalculable kalpas ago, there was a Buddha named Candrasūryapradīpa Tathāgata, Arhat, Samyaksambuddha, Vidyācaraṇasampanna, Sugata, Lokavid, Anuttara, Puruṣadamyasārathi, Śāstā Devamanuṣyāṇām, Buddha, Bhagavān.

He expounded the correct Dharma, good in the beginning, good in the middle, and good in the end, its meaning profound and far-reaching, its words skillful and subtle, pure and unmixed, complete with the marks of pure Brahma conduct. For those seeking to be Śrāvakas, he expounded the Dharma responding to the Four Noble Truths, to cross beyond birth, old age, sickness, and death, to ultimate nirvāṇa. For those seeking to be Pratyekabuddhas, he expounded the Dharma responding to the Twelve Links of Dependent Origination. For Bodhisattvas, he expounded the Dharma responding to the Six Pāramitās, causing them to attain anuttarasamyaksambodhi and accomplish all-knowledge.

Next, there was another Buddha also named Candrasūryapradīpa, and next another Buddha also named Candrasūryapradīpa. In this way, there were twenty thousand Buddhas all with the same name, Candrasūryapradīpa, and also with the same clan name, Bhāradvāja. Maitreya, you should know that from the first Buddha to the last Buddha, all had the same name, Candrasūryapradīpa, complete with the ten titles. The Dharma they expounded was good in the beginning, middle, and end.

Before the last Buddha left home, he had eight princely sons: the first named Mati, the second Sumati, the third Anantamati, the fourth Ratnamati, the fifth Viśeṣamati, the sixth Vimatisamudghātin, the seventh Ghōṣamati, and the eighth Dharmamati. These eight princes possessed sovereign majesty and virtue, each ruling over the four continents. When these princes heard that their father had left home and attained anuttarasamyaksambodhi, they all abandoned the royal throne and likewise left home, generating the Mahāyāna resolve, constantly cultivating Brahma conduct. All became Dharma teachers, having already planted roots of virtue under thousands of millions of Buddhas.

At that time, Candrasūryapradīpa Buddha expounded the Mahāyāna sūtra named Innumerable Meanings, a teaching for Bodhisattvas and guarded in the mindfulness of the Buddhas. Having expounded this sūtra, he immediately sat cross-legged amidst the great assembly and entered the samādhi of the Abode of Innumerable Meanings, his body and mind unmoving. At that time, heaven rained down māndārava flowers, mahā-māndārava flowers, mañjūṣaka flowers, and mahā-mañjūṣaka flowers, scattering them over the Buddha and the great assembly. The universal Buddha-worlds shook in six ways.

Then in the assembly, the bhikṣus, bhikṣuṇīs, upāsakas, upāsikās, devas, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kiṃnaras, mahoragas, humans, non-humans, petty kings, and wheel-turning sage kings—this entire great assembly—attained what they had never experienced before. Joyful with joined palms, they single-mindedly gazed upon the Buddha.

Then the Tathāgata emitted a light from the white hair between his eyebrows, illuminating eighteen thousand Buddha lands to the east without omission, just like all the Buddha lands now seen. Maitreya, you should know that in the assembly at that time, there were twenty billion Bodhisattvas who rejoiced in wishing to hear the Dharma. When these Bodhisattvas saw this light universally illuminating the Buddha lands, they attained what they had never experienced before, wishing to know the causal conditions of this light.

At that time, there was a Bodhisattva named Varaprabha, who had eight hundred disciples. Then Candrasūryapradīpa Buddha arose from samādhi, and on account of Varaprabha Bodhisattva, expounded the Mahāyāna sūtra named the Lotus Flower of the Wonderful Dharma, a teaching for Bodhisattvas and guarded in the mindfulness of the Buddhas. For sixty minor kalpas he did not rise from his seat. The listeners in the assembly also sat in one place for sixty minor kalpas, their bodies and minds unmoving, listening to what the Buddha expounded as if it were the space of a meal. At that time, not a single person in the assembly gave rise to weariness in body or mind.

Candrasūryapradīpa Buddha, having expounded this sūtra for sixty minor kalpas, forthwith in the midst of the Brahmās, Maras, śramaṇas, brāhmaṇas, devas, humans, and asuras, proclaimed these words: Today at midnight, the Tathāgata will enter nirvāṇa without remainder.

At that time, there was a Bodhisattva named Deva-kūṭa; Candrasūryapradīpa Buddha immediately conferred a prophecy upon him, telling the bhikṣus: This Deva-kūṭa Bodhisattva will next become a Buddha, named Śuddhakāya Tathāgata, Arhat, Samyaksambuddha.

After the Buddha conferred the prophecy, at midnight he entered nirvāṇa without remainder. Following the Buddha’s extinction, Varaprabha Bodhisattva upheld the Sūtra of the Lotus Flower of the Wonderful Dharma, expounding it to people for a full eighty minor kalpas. The eight sons of Candrasūryapradīpa Buddha all took Varaprabha as their teacher. Varaprabha taught and transformed them, causing them to become firm in anuttarasamyaksambodhi.

These princes, having made offerings to immeasurable hundreds of thousands of millions of Buddhas, all attained the Buddha path. The last to attain Buddhahood was named Dīpaṃkara.

Among the eight hundred disciples, there was one named Yaśaskāma, who was greedily attached to gain and offerings. Although he repeatedly read and recited various sūtras, he did not penetrate them fluently and forgot much; therefore, he was named Yaśaskāma. Because this person had also planted wholesome roots, he was able to encounter immeasurable hundreds of thousands of millions of Buddhas, making offerings, honoring, respecting, and praising them.

Maitreya, you should know: could Varaprabha Bodhisattva at that time have been someone else? It was I myself; and Yaśaskāma Bodhisattva was you yourself. Now seeing this auspicious sign, it is no different from the original one. Therefore I consider: Today the Tathāgata will expound the Mahāyāna sūtra named the Lotus Flower of the Wonderful Dharma, a teaching for Bodhisattvas and guarded in the mindfulness of the Buddhas.

Then Mañjuśrī, in the midst of the great assembly, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

I recall the past world,

Immeasurable, countless kalpas ago,

There was a Buddha, Honored Among Humans,

Named Candrasūryapradīpa.

The World-Honored One expounded the Dharma,

Liberating immeasurable wandering beings,

And countless millions of Bodhisattvas,

Causing them to enter the Buddha’s wisdom.

Before the Buddha left home,

The eight princes born to him,

Seeing the Great Sage leave home,

Also followed to cultivate Brahma conduct.

Then the Buddha expounded the Great Vehicle,

The sūtra named Innumerable Meanings,

Amidst the great assembly,

Widely distinguishing it for them.

When the Buddha finished speaking this sūtra,

Forthwith upon the Dharma seat,

He sat cross-legged in samādhi,

Named the Abode of Innumerable Meanings.

Heaven rained māndārava flowers,

Heavenly drums naturally sounded,

Devas, Nagas, ghosts, and spirits

Made offerings to the Honored Among Humans.

All the Buddha lands

Immediately shook greatly.

The Buddha emitted light from between his eyebrows,

Displaying various rare matters.

This light illuminated the east,

Eighteen thousand Buddha lands,

Revealing to all wandering beings

The places of their karmic retributions of birth and death.

One saw the Buddha lands

Adorned with various jewels,

Colors of lapis lazuli and crystal,

All illuminated by the Buddha’s light.

And one saw devas, humans,

Nāgas, spirits, yakṣa hosts,

Gandharvas, and kiṃnaras,

Each making offerings to their Buddhas.

Again one saw the Tathāgatas,

Spontaneously attaining the Buddha path,

Their body color like golden mountains,

Upright, majestic, subtle, and sublime.

Like pure lapis lazuli,

Internally revealing an image of pure gold.

The World-Honored One amidst the great assembly

Expounded the profound Dharma meaning.

In each of the Buddha lands,

The Śrāvaka assemblies were countless;

Because of the illumination of the Buddha’s light,

Those great assemblies were fully seen.

There were bhikṣus

Dwelling in mountain forests,

Vigorously upholding pure precepts,

As if guarding a bright pearl.

Again one saw Bodhisattvas

Practicing giving, patience, and the like,

Their numbers like Ganges sands,

All illuminated by the Buddha’s light.

Again one saw Bodhisattvas

Deeply entering various dhyāna meditations,

Body and mind quiet and unmoving,

To seek the unexcelled path.

Again one saw Bodhisattvas

Knowing the mark of tranquil extinction of dharmas,

Each within their own land

Speaking the Dharma to seek the Buddha path.

At that time the fourfold assembly,

Seeing Candrasūryapradīpa Buddha

Display great spiritual powers,

Were all joyful in their minds,

Each asking one another:

For what causal condition is this matter?

The Honored One revered by devas and humans

Just then arose from samādhi,

Praising Varaprabha Bodhisattva:

You are the eye of the world,

The refuge of all,

Able to uphold the Dharma treasury;

The Dharma as I expound it,

Only you can bear witness to and know.

The World-Honored One, having praised him,

Caused Varaprabha to rejoice,

And expounded this Lotus Sūtra,

For sixty minor kalpas,

Without rising from this seat.

The supreme, wonderful Dharma spoken,

This Dharma Teacher Varaprabha

Was completely able to uphold.

The Buddha, having spoken this Lotus

And caused the assembly to rejoice,

Soon after on that very day

Addressed the assembly of devas and humans:

The meaning of the genuine mark of all dharmas

Has already been expounded for you;

I now at midnight

Will enter into nirvāṇa.

Single-mindedly advance with vigor;

You must depart from negligence.

The Buddhas are exceedingly difficult to encounter,

Met once in hundreds of millions of kalpas.

All the sons of the World-Honored One,

Hearing that the Buddha was entering nirvāṇa,

Each harbored grief and distress:

Why is the Buddha’s extinction so swift?

The Holy Lord, King of Dharma,

Comforted the immeasurable assembly:

When I pass into extinction,

Do not harbor sorrow or fear.

This Deva-kūṭa Bodhisattva,

Regarding the uncontaminated genuine mark,

Has already attained penetration in mind.

Next he will become a Buddha,

Named Śuddhakāya,

And will also liberate immeasurable assemblies.

The Buddha that night passed into extinction,

As fire dies out when fuel is exhausted.

They divided his relics

And erected immeasurable stūpas.

Bhikṣus and bhikṣuṇīs,

Their numbers like Ganges sands,

Doubled their vigor

To seek the unexcelled path.

This Dharma Teacher Varaprabha

Upheld the Buddha’s Dharma treasury;

Throughout eighty minor kalpas,

He widely proclaimed the Lotus Sūtra.

All these eight princes,

Taught and transformed by Varaprabha,

Became firm in the unexcelled path

And were to see countless Buddhas.

Having made offerings to the Buddhas,

They followed and practiced the Great Path,

Successively attaining Buddhahood,

In turn receiving prophecies.

The last Deva among Devas

Was named Dīpaṃkara Buddha,

Guide of all sages,

Liberating immeasurable assemblies.

This Dharma Teacher Varaprabha

At that time had a disciple

Whose mind was constantly negligent,

Greedily attached to fame and gain,

Seeking fame and gain without satiety,

Frequently visiting noble families,

Abandoning what he had practiced and recited,

Neglecting and forgetting without penetration.

By this causal condition,

He was named Yaśaskāma.

He also performed many wholesome deeds,

Enabling him to see countless Buddhas,

Making offerings to the Buddhas,

Following and practicing the Great Path,

Fulfilling the Six Pāramitās,

And now beholds the Śākya Lion.

Afterward he will become a Buddha,

Named Maitreya,

Widely liberating wandering beings,

Their numbers having no measure.

After that Buddha passed into extinction,

The negligent one was you;

The Dharma Teacher Varaprabha

Is now none other than myself.

I saw the original auspicious light

Of Candrasūryapradīpa Buddha like this;

By this I know the present Buddha

Wishes to speak the Lotus Sūtra.

The present signs are like the original auspicious signs;

These are the expedient means of the Buddhas.

Now the Buddha radiates light

To help reveal the meaning of the genuine mark.

All people should now know:

Join your palms and single-mindedly wait;

The Buddha will rain down the Dharma rain

To satisfy those who seek the path.

Those who seek the three vehicles,

If they have doubts or regrets,

The Buddha will resolve and sever them,

Causing them to be exhausted without remainder.

Capítulo siguiente →

Capítulo II

Medios hábiles

Este capítulo aún está en traducción al castellano; se muestra provisoriamente en inglés.

At that time, the World-Honored One arose serenely from samādhi and addressed Śāriputra:

The wisdom of the Buddhas is extremely profound and immeasurable. The gate to their wisdom is difficult to understand and difficult to enter; all Śrāvakas and Pratyekabuddhas cannot know it.

For what reason? The Buddhas have attended upon hundreds of thousands of millions of countless Buddhas, fully practicing the immeasurable path of the Buddhas, heroic, vigorous, their names universally renowned, accomplishing the profound, unprecedented Dharma. Expounded according to what is appropriate, its intent is difficult to understand.

Śāriputra! Since I attained Buddhahood, through various causal conditions and various parables, I have widely expounded spoken teachings with countless expedient means, guiding wandering beings to cause them to abandon all attachments. For what reason? The Tathāgata is fully endowed with expedient means and the pāramitā of knowledge and vision.

Śāriputra! The knowledge and vision of the Tathāgata is vast, deep, and far-reaching, immeasurable and unobstructed. His powers, fearlessnesses, dhyāna meditations, and samādhis of liberation deeply enter the boundless, accomplishing all unprecedented dharmas.

Śāriputra! The Tathāgata can distinguish variously and skillfully expound all dharmas, his speech gentle and pleasing the minds of the assembly. Śāriputra! In essence: the Buddha has fully accomplished immeasurable, boundless, unprecedented dharmas.

Stop, Śāriputra! There is no need to speak further. For what reason? What the Buddha has accomplished is the foremost, rare, difficult-to-understand Dharma. Only a Buddha and just a Buddha can exhaust the genuine mark of all dharmas: that is to say, the suchness of the marks of all dharmas, the suchness of their nature, the suchness of their substance, the suchness of their power, the suchness of their function, the suchness of their causes, the suchness of their conditions, the suchness of their effects, the suchness of their retributions, and the suchness of their ultimate equality from beginning to end.

At that time, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

The Hero of the World is immeasurable.

All devas and worldly humans,

All classes of wandering beings,

None can know the Buddha.

The Buddha’s powers, fearlessnesses,

Liberations, and various samādhis,

As well as the Buddha’s other dharmas,

None are able to fathom.

Originally following countless Buddhas,

He fully practiced all paths;

The profound, subtle, and sublime Dharma

Is difficult to see and difficult to understand.

Throughout immeasurable millions of kalpas,

Having practiced these various paths,

Attaining the fruit at the place of awakening,

I have fully known and seen it all.

Such great fruits and retributions,

The meanings of various natures and marks,

Only I and the Buddhas of the ten directions

Are able to know these matters.

This Dharma cannot be demonstrated;

The marks of words are tranquil and extinguished.

All other kinds of wandering beings,

None are able to attain understanding,

Except the assemblies of Bodhisattvas

Whose power of faith is firm.

The assemblies of disciples of the Buddhas,

Who have made offerings to the Buddhas,

With all outpourings exhausted,

Dwelling in this final body—

Such people as these,

Their strength is not equal to it.

Even if the world were full

Of those like Śāriputra,

Exhausting their thoughts to measure it together,

They could not fathom the Buddha’s wisdom.

Even if filling the ten directions,

All were like Śāriputra,

And other disciples

Also filled the lands of the ten directions,

Exhausting their thoughts to measure it together,

They also could not know it.

Pratyekabuddhas of sharp wisdom,

Uncontaminated in their final bodies,

Even if filling the worlds of the ten directions,

Their numbers like bamboo groves,

If these joined together with one mind

Throughout millions of immeasurable kalpas,

Wishing to contemplate the Buddha’s genuine wisdom,

None could know the slightest part.

Newly resolved Bodhisattvas,

Having made offerings to countless Buddhas,

Penetrating all meanings and principles,

And able to skillfully expound the Dharma,

Like rice, hemp, bamboo, and reeds,

Filling the lands of the ten directions,

With one mind and subtle wisdom

Throughout kalpas like Ganges sands,

All reflecting and calculating together,

Could not know the Buddha’s wisdom.

Irreversible Bodhisattvas,

Their numbers like Ganges sands,

With one mind reflecting and seeking together,

Also could not know it.

Furthermore, I address Śāriputra:

This uncontaminated, inconceivable,

Profound, subtle, and sublime Dharma,

I have now fully attained;

Only I know its marks,

And the Buddhas of the ten directions are also thus.

Śāriputra, you must know:

The words of the Buddhas do not differ;

Toward the Dharma expounded by the Buddha,

You must generate great power of faith.

The Dharma of the World-Honored One, after a long time,

Must at last speak the genuine reality.

I address the assemblies of Śrāvakas

And those seeking the Pratyekabuddha vehicle:

I have caused you to be freed from the bonds of suffering

And to attain nirvāṇa.

The Buddha, by the power of expedient means,

Reveals the teachings of the three vehicles;

Wandering beings are attached at every point,

So he guides them to cause them to emerge.

At that time, in the great assembly, there were Śrāvakas, outpourings-exhausted arhats, Ājñātakauṇḍinya and others, twelve hundred persons, as well as bhikṣus, bhikṣuṇīs, upāsakas, and upāsikās who had resolved upon the minds of Śrāvakas and Pratyekabuddhas. Each had this thought: Now, for what reason does the World-Honored One so earnestly praise expedient means and make this statement: “The Dharma attained by the Buddha is profound and difficult to understand; what is spoken has an intent difficult to know, beyond the reach of all Śrāvakas and Pratyekabuddhas”?

The Buddha has spoken of the single meaning of liberation, and we too have attained this Dharma, reaching nirvāṇa. Yet now we do not know where this principle leads.

At that time, Śāriputra, knowing the doubt in the hearts of the fourfold assembly and having not yet understood it himself, addressed the Buddha: World-Honored One! For what cause and condition do you so earnestly praise the foremost expedient means, profound, subtle, and difficult to understand Dharma of the Buddhas? From the past until now, I have never heard such a teaching from the Buddha. Now the fourfold assembly all harbor doubts. We only pray that the World-Honored One will expound this matter. For what reason does the World-Honored One earnestly praise this profound, subtle, and difficult to understand Dharma?

Thereupon, Śāriputra, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

Sun of Wisdom, Great Holy Honored One,

Only after a long time do you expound this Dharma;

You yourself state that you have attained such

Powers, fearlessnesses, and samādhis,

Dhyāna meditations, liberations, and the like,

Inconceivable dharmas.

Regarding the Dharma attained at the place of awakening,

None are able to raise questions;

My intent is difficult to fathom,

And none are able to question it.

Without being questioned, you speak of your own accord,

Praising the path you have walked,

Wisdom exceedingly subtle and sublime,

Attained by all the Buddhas.

All uncontaminated arhats

And those seeking nirvāṇa

Have now fallen into a net of doubt:

Why does the Buddha speak this?

Those who seek Conditioned Awakening,

Bhikṣus and bhikṣuṇīs,

Devas, nāgas, ghosts, spirits,

And gandharvas,

Look at one another harboring hesitation,

Gazing up at the Honored Among Two-Legged Beings.

What is the meaning of this matter?

We pray the Buddha will explain it for us.

Among the assembly of Śrāvakas,

The Buddha declared me foremost;

Now I, regarding my own wisdom,

Am doubtful and unable to understand:

Is this the ultimate Dharma?

Or is it the path to be practiced?

The children born from the Buddha’s mouth,

With palms joined, gaze up and wait;

We pray you will emit the subtle, sublime sound

And in due time speak the reality as it is.

Devas, nāgas, and spirits,

Their numbers like Ganges sands,

The Bodhisattvas seeking Buddhahood,

Numbering eighty thousand,

As well as from tens of billions of lands

Wheel-turning sage kings have arrived,

With joined palms and reverent minds,

Wishing to hear the complete path.

Then the Buddha addressed Śāriputra: Stop, stop! There is no need to speak further. If I speak of this matter, all the world with its devas and humans will be startled and suspicious.

Śāriputra addressed the Buddha again: World-Honored One, we only pray that you will speak it! We only pray that you will speak it! For what reason? In this assembly, there are countless hundreds of thousands of millions of incalculable wandering beings who have seen past Buddhas; their faculties are sharp, their wisdom clear. Hearing what the Buddha expounds, they will be able to revere and believe.

Thereupon, Śāriputra, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

Dharma King, Unexcelled Honored One,

Only speak it, pray do not be anxious.

In this assembly are immeasurable multitudes

Who are able to revere and believe.

The Buddha again stopped Śāriputra: If I speak of this matter, all the world with its devas, humans, and asuras will be startled and suspicious, and bhikṣus of arrogant pride will fall into a great pit.

Then the World-Honored One restated it in gāthās:

Stop, stop, there is no need to speak;

My Dharma is subtle and difficult to conceive.

Those of arrogant pride,

Hearing it, will certainly not revere or believe.

Then Śāriputra addressed the Buddha again: World-Honored One! We only pray that you will speak it! We only pray that you will speak it! Now in this assembly, there are hundreds of thousands of millions like us who, life after life, have received transformation from the Buddha. Such persons as these will certainly be able to revere and believe, resting in peace through the long night, receiving vast benefits.

Thereupon, Śāriputra, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

Unexcelled Honored Among Two-Legged Beings,

Pray speak the foremost Dharma;

I am the eldest child of the Buddha,

Pray deign to distinguish and expound it.

The immeasurable multitudes in this assembly

Can revere and believe this Dharma;

The Buddha has already, life after life,

Taught and transformed such as these.

All with one mind and joined palms

Wish to hear and receive the Buddha’s words.

We twelve hundred,

And others seeking Buddhahood,

Pray for the sake of this assembly,

Deign to distinguish and expound it.

These, hearing this Dharma,

Will then give rise to great joy.

Then the World-Honored One addressed Śāriputra: You have already earnestly requested three times; how can I not speak? Now listen attentively and contemplate it well; I will distinguish and explain it for you.

When this was said, there were five thousand bhikṣus, bhikṣuṇīs, upāsakas, and upāsikās in the assembly who immediately rose from their seats, bowed to the Buddha, and withdrew. For what reason? The roots of transgression of this group were deep and heavy, and their arrogant pride was great; not having attained, they claimed to have attained; not having realized, they claimed to have realized. Having such faults, they did not remain. The World-Honored One remained silent and did not restrain them.

Then the Buddha addressed Śāriputra: My assembly is now free of branches and leaves; only pure truth remains. Śāriputra! It is good that such persons of arrogant pride have withdrawn. Listen well now; I will speak it for you.

Śāriputra said: Yes, World-Honored One! We joyfully wish to hear it.

The Buddha addressed Śāriputra: Such a wonderful Dharma as this is spoken by the Buddhas, the Tathāgatas, only at certain times, just like the uḍumbara flower appears but once in a long age. Śāriputra! You must believe what the Buddha expounds; his words are not false.

Śāriputra! The Buddhas expound the Dharma according to what is appropriate; its intent is difficult to understand. For what reason? I, by countless expedient means, various causal conditions, parables, and words, expound all dharmas. This Dharma cannot be understood through thought and discrimination; only Buddhas can know it.

For what reason? The Buddhas, the World-Honored Ones, appear in the world only for the sake of one great causal condition.

Śāriputra! What is meant by: the Buddhas, the World-Honored Ones, appear in the world only for the sake of one great causal condition?

The Buddhas, the World-Honored Ones, wishing to cause wandering beings to open the Buddha’s knowledge and vision so that they may attain purity, appear in the world.

Wishing to reveal the Buddha’s knowledge and vision to wandering beings, they appear in the world. Wishing to cause wandering beings to awaken to the Buddha’s knowledge and vision, they appear in the world. Wishing to cause wandering beings to enter the path of the Buddha’s knowledge and vision, they appear in the world.

Śāriputra! This is the one great causal condition for which the Buddhas appear in the world.

The Buddha addressed Śāriputra: The Buddhas, the Tathāgatas, teach and transform only Bodhisattvas. All their actions are constantly for this one thing: solely with the Buddha’s knowledge and vision do they reveal and awaken wandering beings.

Śāriputra! The Tathāgata, solely by the One Buddha Vehicle, expounds the Dharma for wandering beings; there are no other vehicles, neither two nor three. Śāriputra! The Dharma of all Buddhas of the ten directions is also like this.

Śāriputra! The Buddhas of the past, with immeasurable, countless expedient means, various causal conditions, parables, and words, expounded all dharmas for wandering beings; these dharmas were all for the sake of the One Buddha Vehicle. These wandering beings, hearing the Dharma from the Buddhas, ultimately all attain all-knowledge.

Śāriputra! The Buddhas of the future who will appear in the world will also, with immeasurable, countless expedient means, various causal conditions, parables, and words, expound all dharmas for wandering beings; these dharmas are all for the sake of the One Buddha Vehicle. These wandering beings, hearing the Dharma from the Buddhas, will ultimately all attain all-knowledge.

Śāriputra! The present Buddhas, the World-Honored Ones, in immeasurable hundreds of thousands of millions of Buddha lands of the ten directions, extensively benefit and bring peace and joy to wandering beings; these Buddhas also, with immeasurable, countless expedient means, various causal conditions, parables, and words, expound all dharmas for wandering beings; these dharmas are all for the sake of the One Buddha Vehicle. These wandering beings, hearing the Dharma from the Buddhas, ultimately all attain all-knowledge.

Śāriputra! These Buddhas only teach and transform Bodhisattvas, wishing to reveal the Buddha’s knowledge and vision to wandering beings, wishing to awaken wandering beings to the Buddha’s knowledge and vision, wishing to cause wandering beings to enter the Buddha’s knowledge and vision.

Śāriputra! I now am also like this. Knowing that wandering beings have various desires deeply attached in their hearts, according to their original natures, by various causal conditions, parables, words, and the power of expedient means, I expound the Dharma for them. Śāriputra! All this is done in order to attain the One Buddha Vehicle and all-knowledge.

Śāriputra! In the worlds of the ten directions, there are not even two vehicles, much less three.

Śāriputra! The Buddhas appear in the evil world of the five turbidities: namely, kalpa turbidity, affliction turbidity, wandering being turbidity, view turbidity, and life turbidity. Thus, Śāriputra, during the chaotic time of kalpa turbidity, the defilements of wandering beings are heavy; stingy, greedy, and envious, they accomplish various unwholesome roots. Therefore, the Buddhas, by the power of expedient means, within the One Buddha Vehicle, distinguish and speak of three.

Śāriputra! If my disciples claim themselves to be arhats or pratyekabuddhas, yet do not hear and do not know that the Buddhas, the Tathāgatas, solely teach and transform Bodhisattvas, they are not disciples of the Buddha, not arhats, and not pratyekabuddhas.

Furthermore, Śāriputra! These bhikṣus and bhikṣuṇīs who claim they have already attained arhatship, that this is their final body and ultimate nirvāṇa, and thus no longer aspire to seek anuttarasamyaksambodhi—you must know that these people are all of arrogant pride.

For what reason? If there is a bhikṣu who has truly attained arhatship, if he does not believe this Dharma, there is no such possibility—except after the Buddha’s extinction, when no Buddha is present. For what reason? After the Buddha’s extinction, those who uphold, read, recite, and understand the meaning of such sūtras as this are difficult to find. If they encounter another Buddha, they will then attain clear resolution in this Dharma.

Śāriputra! You all should single-mindedly believe, understand, receive, and uphold the Buddha’s words. The words of the Buddhas, the Tathāgatas, are without falsehood; there is no other vehicle, only the One Buddha Vehicle.

Thereupon, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

Bhikṣus and bhikṣuṇīs

Harboring arrogant pride,

Upāsakas with self-grasping pride,

Upāsikās lacking faith—

Such members of the fourfold assembly,

Five thousand in number,

Do not see their own errors,

Having flaws in their morality,

Guarding their own defects.

These of small wisdom have already left;

The chaff among the assembly

Departed through the Buddha’s majesty and virtue.

These persons have meager merit;

They cannot receive this Dharma.

This assembly has no branches and leaves,

Possessing only pure reality.

Śāriputra, listen well!

The Dharma attained by the Buddhas,

By immeasurable power of expedient means,

Is expounded for wandering beings.

The thoughts in the minds of wandering beings,

The various paths they practice,

Their diverse desires and natures,

Their wholesome and unwholesome karma of past lives—

The Buddha, fully knowing all these,

Uses various conditions, parables,

Words, and the power of expedient means,

To cause them all to rejoice.

He expounds sūtras,

Gāthās, and itivṛttakas,

Jātakas, and adbhutadharmas,

He also expounds nidānas,

Avadānas, and geyas,

As well as upadeśa scriptures.

Those of dull faculties who delight in small dharmas,

Greedily attached to birth and death,

Under immeasurable Buddhas

Do not practice the deep, subtle path;

Distressed by many sufferings,

For their sake he speaks of nirvāṇa.

I devised this expedient means

To cause them to enter the Buddha’s wisdom;

I never said to you

That you will attain the Buddha path.

The reason I did not say it before

Was that the time to speak had not yet arrived;

Now is precisely the time

To definitively speak the Great Vehicle.

These nine divisions of Dharma of mine,

Expounded in accord with wandering beings,

Have entering the Great Vehicle as their root;

Therefore this sūtra is expounded.

There are Buddha-children with pure minds,

Gentle, and of sharp faculties,

Who under immeasurable Buddhas

Have practiced the deep, subtle path.

For these Buddha-children,

I expound this Great Vehicle sūtra;

I predict that such persons as these

Will in future lives attain the Buddha path,

Because with deep minds they are mindful of the Buddha

And cultivate pure morality.

These, hearing that they will attain Buddhahood,

Will have great joy pervade their bodies;

The Buddha, knowing their mental actions,

Therefore expounds the Great Vehicle for them.

Whether Śrāvakas or Bodhisattvas,

Hearing the Dharma I expound,

Even down to a single gāthā,

All will attain Buddhahood without doubt.

Within the Buddha lands of the ten directions,

There is only the Dharma of the One Vehicle,

There are neither two nor three,

Except for the expedient words of the Buddhas.

Merely using provisional names

To guide wandering beings,

Speaking of the Buddha’s wisdom,

The Buddhas appear in the world.

Only this one matter is real;

The other two are not genuine;

Never by the Small Vehicle

Does he liberate wandering beings.

The Buddha himself dwells in the Great Vehicle;

According to the Dharma he attained,

Adorned with the power of meditation and wisdom,

By this he liberates wandering beings.

Having realized the unexcelled path himself,

The equal Dharma of the Great Vehicle,

If he transformed through the Small Vehicle,

Even down to a single person,

I would fall into stinginess and greed;

This matter cannot be.

If a person faithfully takes refuge in the Buddha,

The Tathāgata does not deceive them,

Nor has he thoughts of greed or jealousy;

He severs evil from among all dharmas.

Therefore the Buddha in the ten directions

Is uniquely free of fear.

Adorning my body with marks,

My radiant light illuminates the world;

Revered by immeasurable multitudes,

I speak for them the seal of the genuine mark.

Śāriputra, you must know:

I originally made a vow,

Wishing to cause all multitudes

To be equal to me without difference.

As I originally vowed in the past,

Now it has already been fulfilled;

Transforming all wandering beings,

I cause them all to enter the Buddha path.

If I encounter wandering beings,

Teaching them entirely with the Buddha path,

Those without wisdom, confused and errant,

Deluded, will not receive the teaching.

I know these wandering beings

Have never cultivated roots of virtue,

Firmly attached to the five desires,

Giving rise to afflictions through foolish attachment.

By causal conditions of various desires,

They fall into the three evil destinies,

Transmigrating within the six realms,

Fully undergoing painful torments,

Taking subtle shapes within the womb,

Increasing life after life.

Persons of meager virtue and little merit,

Oppressed by many sufferings,

Enter the dense forest of false views,

Such as existence or non-existence.

Relying on these various views,

Complete in sixty-two varieties,

Deeply attached to false dharmas,

Holding firmly and unable to let go,

Haughty with self-grasping pride,

Flattering, crooked, with ungenuine minds,

Through thousands of millions of kalpas,

They do not hear the name of the Buddha,

Nor do they hear the correct Dharma;

Such persons as these are difficult to liberate.

Therefore, Śāriputra!

I establish expedient means for them,

Speaking of the path that ends suffering,

Showing them nirvāṇa.

Although I speak of nirvāṇa,

This is also not genuine extinction;

All dharmas from the very beginning

Are constantly of the mark of tranquil extinction.

When Buddha-children have practiced the path,

In future lives they will become Buddhas;

I possess the power of expedient means

To open and reveal the Dharma of the three vehicles.

All the World-Honored Ones

All speak the path of the One Vehicle.

Now this great assembly

Should completely eliminate doubts;

The words of the Buddhas do not differ:

There is only the One, no second vehicle.

Throughout countless kalpas of the past,

Immeasurable Buddhas who passed into extinction,

Hundreds of thousands of millions of kinds,

Their numbers beyond calculation—

Such World-Honored Ones as these,

With various conditions and parables,

And countless powers of expedient means,

Expounded the marks of all dharmas.

These World-Honored Ones

All spoke the Dharma of the One Vehicle,

Transforming immeasurable wandering beings

To cause them to enter the Buddha path.

Furthermore, the Great Holy Lords,

Knowing what all in the world,

Devas, humans, and groups of living beings,

Desire deep within their hearts,

Further used distinct expedient means

To help illuminate the primary truth.

If there were classes of wandering beings

Who met past Buddhas,

If they heard the Dharma and practiced giving,

Or upheld precepts, patience,

Vigor, dhyāna, and wisdom,

Variously cultivating merit and wisdom—

Such persons as these

Have all attained the Buddha path.

After the Buddhas passed into extinction,

If a person had a wholesome, gentle mind,

Such wandering beings as these

Have all attained the Buddha path.

After the Buddhas passed into extinction,

Those who made offerings to relics,

Erecting thousands of millions of stūpas,

Of gold, silver, and crystal,

Mother-of-pearl and agate,

Rose quartz, lapis lazuli, and pearls,

Purely and widely adorned,

Decorating these stūpas;

Or those who erected stone shrines,

Of sandalwood and aloeswood,

Deodar and other timber,

Bricks, tiles, clay, and earth;

Or if in an open wilderness,

They heaped earth to make a Buddha shrine,

Even down to children at play

Gathering sand to make a Buddha stūpa—

Such persons as these

Have all attained the Buddha path.

If a person, for the Buddha’s sake,

Erected various images,

Carving and sculpting them into various forms,

They have all attained the Buddha path.

Or if made of the seven treasures,

Brass, red and white copper,

Pewter, lead, and tin,

Iron, wood, and clay,

Or with lacquered cloth,

Adorning and making Buddha statues—

Such persons as these

Have all attained the Buddha path.

Those who painted Buddha images,

Adorned with the marks of a hundred merits,

Whether painted themselves or having others do so,

Have all attained the Buddha path.

Even down to children at play,

With blades of grass, wood, or brushes,

Or with their own fingernails,

Drawing Buddha images—

Such persons as these,

Gradually accumulating merits,

Complete with the great compassionate mind,

Have all attained the Buddha path.

Merely transforming the Bodhisattvas,

They liberate immeasurable multitudes.

If a person, at stūpas and shrines,

Before precious images or painted images,

With flowers, incense, banners, and canopies,

Reverently makes offerings;

Or causes people to make music,

Striking drums, blowing horns and conches,

Flutes, pipes, harps, lutes,

Pīpās, cymbals, and gongs,

Such wondrous sounds as these,

All offered up as worship;

Or with a joyful mind,

Sings hymns in praise of the Buddha’s virtues,

Even down to a single faint sound,

They have all attained the Buddha path.

If a person with distracted mind,

Even with a single flower,

Makes an offering to a painted image,

They will gradually see countless Buddhas.

Or if a person bows down in homage,

Or merely joins palms,

Even raising a single hand,

Or slightly bowing the head,

By this offering to the image,

They will gradually see immeasurable Buddhas.

Themselves attaining the unexcelled path,

They will widely liberate countless multitudes,

Entering nirvāṇa without remainder,

As fire dies out when fuel is exhausted.

If a person with distracted mind

Enters into a stūpa or shrine,

And utters but once: “Homage to the Buddha!”

They have all attained the Buddha path.

Under the past Buddhas,

Whether while they were in the world or after their extinction,

If there were those who heard this Dharma,

They have all attained the Buddha path.

The future World-Honored Ones,

Their numbers having no measure,

These Tathāgatas

Will also expound the Dharma through expedient means.

All the Tathāgatas,

With immeasurable expedient means,

Liberate wandering beings,

Causing them to enter the Buddha’s uncontaminated wisdom.

If there are those who hear the Dharma,

Not one will fail to attain Buddhahood.

The original vow of the Buddhas,

The Buddha path that I practice,

I wish universally to cause wandering beings

Also to attain this same path.

Although the Buddhas of future ages

Will speak hundreds of thousands of millions

Of countless Dharma gates,

In reality they are for the One Vehicle.

The Buddhas, Honored Among Two-Legged Beings,

Know that dharmas are ever without inherent nature;

The Buddha-seeds arise from conditions;

Therefore they speak the One Vehicle.

These dharmas abide in their Dharma-positions;

The worldly marks are ever-abiding.

Having known this at the place of awakening,

The Guides expound it through expedient means.

Revered by devas and humans,

The present Buddhas of the ten directions,

Their numbers like Ganges sands,

Appearing within the world

To bring peace and safety to wandering beings,

Also speak such a Dharma as this.

Knowing the foremost tranquil extinction,

Through the power of expedient means,

Although they display various paths,

In reality they are for the Buddha Vehicle.

Knowing the conduct of wandering beings,

The thoughts deep within their hearts,

The karma practiced in the past,

Their desires, natures, and powers of vigor,

As well as whether faculties are sharp or dull,

By various causal conditions,

Parables, and words,

They speak according to what is appropriate through expedient means.

Now I am also like this;

To bring peace and safety to wandering beings,

Through various Dharma gates,

I proclaim the Buddha path.

By the power of my wisdom,

Knowing the natures and desires of wandering beings,

I expound all dharmas through expedient means,

Causing them all to attain joy.

Śāriputra, you must know!

Observing with the Buddha eye,

I see wandering beings in the six destinies,

Impoverished, lacking merit and wisdom,

Entering the perilous path of birth and death,

Their sufferings continuing without break,

Deeply attached to the five desires,

Like a yak cherishing its tail,

Blinding themselves with greed and love,

Blind and dark, seeing nothing.

They do not seek the Buddha of great power,

Nor the Dharma that severs suffering,

Entering deeply into false views,

Wishing to shed suffering through suffering;

For the sake of these wandering beings,

I give rise to a great compassionate mind.

When I first sat at the place of awakening,

Gazing at the tree and walking about,

Throughout three times seven days,

I contemplated such matters as this:

The wisdom I have attained

Is subtle, sublime, and foremost.

Wandering beings have dull faculties,

Attached to pleasure, blinded by delusion;

Classes of beings such as these,

How could they be liberated?

At that time all the Brahmā Kings,

As well as Śakra, Lord of Devas,

The Four Heavenly Kings guarding the world,

And the Great Sovereign Deva Maheśvara,

Together with the other assemblies of devas,

Attendants numbering hundreds of thousands of millions,

Reverently joined palms and bowed,

Requesting me to turn the wheel of Dharma.

I then thought to myself:

If I merely praise the Buddha Vehicle,

Wandering beings submerged in suffering

Will not be able to believe this Dharma;

Because they contravene the Dharma and do not believe,

They will fall into the three evil destinies.

Better that I not speak the Dharma

And quickly enter into nirvāṇa.

Then I recalled the past Buddhas,

The power of expedient means they practiced;

The path I have now attained

Should also be expounded as three vehicles.

When I had this thought,

The Buddhas of the ten directions all appeared,

Comforting me with Brahma-sounds:

Excellent, Śākyamuni!

Foremost Guide,

Having attained this unexcelled Dharma,

Following all the Buddhas,

You will use the power of expedient means.

We too have all attained

The most wondrous, foremost Dharma.

For the sake of classes of wandering beings,

We distinguish and speak of three vehicles.

Those of small wisdom delight in small dharmas,

Not believing they themselves will become Buddhas;

Therefore through expedient means,

We distinguish and speak of various fruits.

Although they speak of three vehicles,

It is only for the sake of teaching Bodhisattvas.

Śāriputra, you must know!

When I heard the holy lions’

Deep, pure, subtle, and sublime sounds,

Joyfully I proclaimed: Homage to the Buddhas!

Again I had this thought:

I have appeared in a turbid and evil world;

As the Buddhas have spoken,

I too will follow and act accordingly.

Having contemplated this matter,

I immediately set out for Vārāṇasī.

The marks of tranquil extinction of all dharmas

Cannot be proclaimed in words;

Through the power of expedient means,

I expounded it for the five bhikṣus.

This is named the turning of the wheel of Dharma.

Then there were the sounds of nirvāṇa,

As well as of arhat,

And distinct names of Dharma and Saṅgha.

From distant kalpas onward,

Praising and showing the Dharma of nirvāṇa,

Birth and death’s suffering forever exhausted—

I have constantly spoken thus.

Śāriputra, you must know!

I see the Buddha-children

Aspiring to seek the Buddha path,

Immeasurable thousands of millions,

All with reverent minds,

All coming to where the Buddha dwells,

Having heard from the Buddhas

The Dharma expounded through expedient means.

I immediately had this thought:

The reason the Tathāgata appears

Is to speak the Buddha’s wisdom;

Now is precisely the proper time.

Śāriputra, you must know!

People of dull faculties and small wisdom,

Attached to marks, proud and arrogant,

Cannot believe this Dharma.

Now I, joyful and fearless,

Amidst the Bodhisattvas,

Directly abandon expedient means,

Speaking only the unexcelled path.

When Bodhisattvas hear this Dharma,

Their nets of doubts are all eliminated;

The twelve hundred arhats

Will also all become Buddhas.

As is the ritual manner of speaking the Dharma

Of the Buddhas of the three periods of time,

I now am also like this,

Speaking the Dharma free of discrimination.

The Buddhas’ appearing in the world

Is distant and difficult to encounter;

Even when appearing in the world,

Speaking this Dharma is also difficult.

Through immeasurable, countless kalpas,

Hearing this Dharma is also difficult;

Those persons who can listen to this Dharma,

Such persons are also rare.

Just like the uḍumbara flower,

Loved and delighted in by all,

A rarity to devas and humans,

Appearing once in a great while.

If one hears the Dharma, rejoices and praises it,

Even uttering but a single word,

One has already made offerings

To all Buddhas of the three periods of time;

Such a person is exceedingly rare,

Surpassing even the uḍumbara flower.

Do not harbor any doubts;

I am the King of all dharmas.

I universally announce to the great assembly:

Solely by the path of the One Vehicle

Do I teach and transform Bodhisattvas;

There are no Śrāvaka disciples.

You, Śāriputra,

Both Śrāvakas and Bodhisattvas,

Should know that this wonderful Dharma

Is the secret essential of all Buddhas.

In the evil world of the five turbidities,

Beings merely delight in and are attached to desires;

Such wandering beings as these

Ultimately do not seek the Buddha path.

Evil people in future ages,

Hearing the Buddha speak the One Vehicle,

Deluded, will not believe or receive it;

Contravening the Dharma, they will fall into evil destinies.

For those with shame, remorse, and purity,

Who aspire to seek the Buddha path,

For such as these,

You should widely praise the path of the One Vehicle.

Śāriputra, you must know!

The Dharma of the Buddhas is like this:

With tens of billions of expedient means,

Expounding the Dharma according to what is appropriate;

Those who do not practice and study it

Cannot understand this.

Since you all already know

That the Buddhas, the Teachers of the World,

Act through appropriate expedient means,

You will have no further doubts;

With great joy arising in your hearts,

You know that you will certainly become Buddhas.

← Capítulo anteriorCapítulo siguiente →

Capítulo III

Parábolas

Este capítulo aún está en traducción al castellano; se muestra provisoriamente en inglés.

At that time, Śāriputra leaped up in joy. Immediately rising, he joined his palms, gazed up at the venerable countenance, and addressed the Buddha: Now, hearing this Dharma sound from the World-Honored One, my heart leaps with joy, having attained what I never experienced before.

For what reason? In the past, I heard such Dharma from the Buddha and saw the Bodhisattvas receive prophecies of attaining Buddhahood, yet we were not included in this matter. I deeply grieved and lamented losing the Tathāgata’s immeasurable knowledge and vision.

World-Honored One! I was constantly dwelling alone in mountain forests or under trees, whether sitting or walking, and always had this thought: “We equally enter into Dharma-nature. Why does the Tathāgata liberate us through the Small Vehicle?” This was our own fault, not that of the World-Honored One.

For what reason? If we had waited for the explanation of the causes for accomplishing anuttarasamyaksambodhi, we would certainly have attained liberation through the Great Vehicle. Yet we did not understand the expedient means expounded according to what is appropriate. Upon first hearing the Buddha-Dharma, encountering it, we immediately believed, accepted, contemplated, and realized it.

World-Honored One! From the past onward, throughout the day and through the night, I constantly blamed myself. Yet now, hearing from the Buddha what I had never heard before, this unprecedented Dharma, I have severed all doubts and regrets. My body and mind are at ease, happily attaining peaceful security. Today I finally know that I am truly a Buddha-child, born from the Buddha’s mouth, born from the transformation of the Dharma, obtaining a share of the Buddha-Dharma.

Thereupon, Śāriputra, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

Hearing this sound of Dharma,

I have attained what I never had before;

My heart harbors great joy,

The net of doubts is completely eliminated.

From the past, receiving the Buddha’s teaching,

I have not lost the Great Vehicle.

The Buddha’s sound is exceedingly rare,

Able to eliminate the afflictions of wandering beings;

I have already attained the exhaustion of outpourings,

Yet hearing it also eliminates grief and affliction.

Dwelling in mountain valleys,

Or beneath forest trees,

Whether sitting or walking,

I constantly contemplated this matter,

Alas, deeply reproaching myself:

Why have I deceived myself?

We too are Buddha-children,

Equally entering uncontaminated dharmas,

Yet unable in future ages

To proclaim the unexcelled path.

The golden color, the thirty-two marks,

The ten powers, and the liberations,

Together within the single Dharma,

Yet we did not attain these matters.

The eighty subtle marks,

The eighteen distinct dharmas,

Virtues and merits such as these,

I have completely lost.

When walking alone,

I saw the Buddha amidst the great assembly,

His fame filling the ten directions,

Extensively benefiting wandering beings.

Reflecting on having lost this benefit,

I considered that I was deceiving myself.

Constantly, through day and night,

I always contemplated this matter,

Wishing to ask the World-Honored One:

Have I lost it or not?

I constantly saw the World-Honored One

Praising the Bodhisattvas;

Therefore, through day and night,

I pondered matters such as this.

Now hearing the Buddha’s voice,

Expounding the Dharma according to what is appropriate,

Uncontaminated and difficult to conceive,

Leading the assembly to the place of awakening.

Originally attached to false views,

I was a master to brahmacārins;

The World-Honored One, knowing my mind,

Uprooted the false and spoke of nirvāṇa.

Completely eliminating false views,

I attained realization in the Dharma of emptiness.

At that time, I considered in my mind

That I had reached tranquil extinction;

Yet now I realize

That it was not genuine extinction.

If one can become a Buddha,

Complete with the thirty-two marks,

Revered by devas, humans, yakṣa hosts,

Nāgas, and spirits,

Only at that time can it be said

To be eternal extinction without remainder.

The Buddha amidst the great assembly

Proclaims that I will become a Buddha;

Hearing such a sound of Dharma,

Doubts and regrets are completely eliminated.

When first hearing what the Buddha spoke,

In my heart was great alarm and doubt:

Could it be that Mara is acting as the Buddha,

Disturbing and confusing my mind?

The Buddha, with various conditions,

Parables, and skillful words,

With mind serene as the ocean,

Spoke, and my net of doubts was severed.

The Buddha spoke of past eras,

Immeasurable Buddhas who entered extinction,

Dwelling peacefully in expedient means,

Who also all spoke this Dharma.

The Buddhas of the present and future,

Their numbers having no measure,

Also by various expedient means

Expound such Dharma as this.

Just as the present World-Honored One,

From birth and leaving home,

Attaining the path and turning the Dharma wheel,

Also speaks through expedient means.

The World-Honored One speaks the genuine path;

Pāpīyān has no such matter.

By this I definitively know

That it is not Mara acting as the Buddha.

Because I fell into a net of doubts,

I thought it was the work of Mara;

Hearing the Buddha’s gentle sound,

Profound, far-reaching, subtle, and sublime,

Expressing the pure Dharma,

My mind rejoiced greatly.

Doubts and regrets are forever exhausted,

Dwelling peacefully in genuine wisdom.

I will definitively become a Buddha,

Revered by devas and humans,

Turning the unexcelled wheel of Dharma,

Teaching and transforming Bodhisattvas.

Then the Buddha addressed Śāriputra: I now proclaim amidst the great assembly of devas, humans, śramaṇas, and brāhmaṇas: in the past, under twenty thousand billion Buddhas, for the sake of the unexcelled path, I constantly taught and transformed you. You also, throughout the long night, followed me to receive the teachings.

Because I guided you through expedient means, you were born into my Dharma. Śāriputra! In the past, I taught you to resolve upon the Buddha path. You have now completely forgotten this, and thus claimed that you had already attained extinction. Now, wishing to cause you to recall the path of your original vow, for the sake of the Śrāvakas I expound this Great Vehicle sūtra named the Lotus Flower of the Wonderful Dharma, a teaching for Bodhisattvas and guarded in the mindfulness of the Buddhas.

Śāriputra! In a future age, after immeasurable, boundless, inconceivable kalpas have passed, having made offerings to several tens of billions of Buddhas, having upheld the correct Dharma, and having perfected the path practiced by Bodhisattvas, you will become a Buddha named Padmaprabha Tathāgata, Arhat, Samyaksambuddha, Vidyācaraṇasampanna, Sugata, Lokavid, Anuttara, Puruṣadamyasārathi, Śāstā Devamanuṣyāṇām, Buddha, Bhagavān.

His land will be named Virajā. That land will be level and upright, pure and adorned, peaceful, abundant, and joyful, flourishing with devas and humans. Lapis lazuli will form the ground, with eight intersecting roadways bordered with gold cords. Along their sides will stand rows of trees of the seven treasures, constantly bearing flowers and fruits. Padmaprabha Tathāgata will also teach and transform wandering beings through the three vehicles.

Śāriputra! When that Buddha appears, although it will not be an evil world, because of his original vow, he will expound the Dharma of the three vehicles. His kalpa will be named Mahāratnapratimaṇḍita. For what reason will it be named Mahāratnapratimaṇḍita? Because in that land, Bodhisattvas will be regarded as the great treasures.

Those Bodhisattvas will be immeasurable, boundless, and inconceivable, beyond the reach of calculation or parables; none can know them except through the power of the Buddha’s wisdom. When they wish to walk, jewel flowers will receive their feet. These Bodhisattvas will not be newly resolved; they will have long planted roots of virtue, purely cultivating Brahma conduct under immeasurable hundreds of thousands of millions of Buddhas, constantly praised by the Buddhas. Always cultivating the Buddha’s wisdom, endowed with great spiritual superknowledges, well understanding the gates of all dharmas, upright and unfeigned, their resolve and mindfulness firm: such Bodhisattvas will fill that land.

Śāriputra! The lifespan of Padmaprabha Buddha will be twelve minor kalpas, excluding the time before becoming a Buddha while still a prince. The lifespan of the people of that land will be eight minor kalpas.

After twelve minor kalpas have passed, Padmaprabha Tathāgata will confer upon Dhṛtiparipūrṇa Bodhisattva the prophecy of anuttarasamyaksambodhi, addressing the bhikṣus: “This Dhṛtiparipūrṇa Bodhisattva will next become a Buddha, named Padmacaraṇasupratiṣṭhita Tathāgata, Arhat, Samyaksambuddha. His Buddha land will also be like this.”

Śāriputra! After this Padmaprabha Buddha passes into extinction, the Correct Dharma will abide in the world for thirty-two minor kalpas; the Semblance Dharma will also abide in the world for thirty-two minor kalpas.

Thereupon, the World-Honored One, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

Śāriputra in a future life

Will become a Buddha, the Universally Wise Honored One,

Named Padmaprabha,

Who will liberate immeasurable multitudes.

Making offerings to countless Buddhas,

Perfecting the Bodhisattva practices,

Endowed with the ten powers and other merits,

He will realize the unexcelled path.

After immeasurable kalpas have passed,

The kalpa named Mahāratnapratimaṇḍita,

The world named Virajā,

Pure and free of flaws and defilements.

With lapis lazuli as its ground,

Golden cords bordering its roads,

Variegated trees of the seven treasures,

Constantly bearing flowers and fruits.

The Bodhisattvas in that land,

With resolve and mindfulness ever firm,

Endowed with spiritual powers and pāramitās,

All completely fulfilled.

Under countless Buddhas,

Skillfully learning the Bodhisattva path,

Such great beings as these

Will be transformed by Padmaprabha Buddha.

When the Buddha was a prince,

Abandoning the kingdom and worldly glory,

In his final bodily existence,

He left home and attained the Buddha path.

Padmaprabha Buddha dwelling in the world

Will have a lifespan of twelve minor kalpas;

The multitude of people in that land

Will have lifespans of eight minor kalpas.

After the Buddha passes into extinction,

The Correct Dharma will abide in the world

For thirty-two minor kalpas,

Widely liberating wandering beings.

When the Correct Dharma has ended,

The Semblance Dharma will abide for thirty-two;

His relics widely distributed,

Devas and humans universally making offerings.

The deeds of Padmaprabha Buddha,

His activities will all be like this;

That holy Honored One Among Two-Legged Beings

Will be supreme and without equal.

He is none other than yourself;

You should rejoice and celebrate.

At that time, the fourfold assembly of bhikṣus, bhikṣuṇīs, upāsakas, and upāsikās, along with devas, nāgas, yakṣas, gandharvas, asuras, garuḍas, kiṃnaras, and mahoragas, seeing Śāriputra receive the prophecy of anuttarasamyaksambodhi before the Buddha, were filled with great joy and leaped boundlessly. Each removed their upper garments to offer them to the Buddha.

Śakra Devānām Indra, Brahmā Kings, and countless sons of devas also offered heavenly subtle garments, heavenly māndārava flowers, and mahā-māndārava flowers to the Buddha. The scattered heavenly robes remained suspended in open space, whirling about of themselves. Hundreds of thousands of kinds of heavenly instruments sounded simultaneously in open space, and a rain of heavenly flowers fell, as they said: In the past at Vārāṇasī, the Buddha first turned the wheel of Dharma; now he turns again the unexcelled, greatest wheel of Dharma!

Thereupon, the sons of devas, wishing to restate this meaning, spoke gāthās:

In the past at Vārāṇasī,

You turned the Dharma wheel of the Four Truths,

Distinguishing and expounding all dharmas,

The arising and ceasing of the five aggregates.

Now you turn again the most wondrous,

Unexcelled, great wheel of Dharma;

This Dharma is exceedingly profound and abstruse,

Few are able to believe it.

From the past, we

Have frequently heard the World-Honored One speak,

Yet never heard such

Profound, subtle, and supreme Dharma.

When the World-Honored One speaks this Dharma,

We all rejoice in accord.

The great wise Śāriputra

Now receives the honored prophecy;

We too are like this,

And will certainly become Buddhas.

In all the worlds,

Most honored and without superior,

The Buddha path is inconceivable,

Expounded through expedient means as appropriate.

Whatever meritorious karma we possess,

In this life or past lives,

And the merit of beholding the Buddha,

We dedicate entirely to the Buddha path.

At that time, Śāriputra addressed the Buddha: World-Honored One! I now have no further doubts or regrets, having personally received the prophecy of anuttarasamyaksambodhi before the Buddha. Yet these twelve hundred whose minds have attained mastery previously dwelt on the level of training, and the Buddha constantly taught and transformed them, saying: “My Dharma can lead away from birth, old age, sickness, and death, to ultimate nirvāṇa.”

These persons in training and beyond training also each considered that having abandoned the self-view, the view of existence, the view of non-existence, and the like, they had attained nirvāṇa. Now, hearing before the World-Honored One what they had never heard before, they have all fallen into doubt and hesitation. Excellent, World-Honored One! We pray you will speak the causal conditions for the sake of the fourfold assembly, causing them to abandon doubts and regrets.

Then the Buddha addressed Śāriputra: Did I not say before: “The Buddhas, the World-Honored Ones, expound the Dharma through various causal conditions, parables, words, and expedient means, all for the sake of anuttarasamyaksambodhi”? All these teachings are for the sake of transforming Bodhisattvas.

However, Śāriputra! I will now use a parable to further clarify this meaning. Those who have wisdom attain understanding through parables.

Śāriputra! Suppose in a country, city, or village there is a great elder, advanced in years, of immeasurable wealth, possessing many fields, dwellings, and servants. His mansion is vast and extensive, with only a single gate. Many people—one hundred, two hundred, or even five hundred persons—dwell within it.

The halls and chambers are decaying and old, the walls crumbling, the bases of the pillars rotted, the beams and ridgepoles tilted and precarious. Suddenly, all at once on every side, a fire breaks out, burning the dwelling.

The sons of the elder—ten, twenty, or up to thirty—are inside this dwelling. The elder, seeing this great fire erupting from all four directions, is immediately startled and terrified, thinking: “Although I can safely escape through this burning gate, my children inside the burning house are joyfully attached to games and amusements. Unaware and uncomprehending, not startled and not terrified, the fire approaches pressing upon their bodies, sharp suffering cutting close, yet their hearts feel no dread, having no intention of seeking escape.”
